      14              : /*
      15              : The following section attempts to detect the compiler and version in use.

      18              : they take responsibility for setting any compiler-dependent macros
      19              : used later in the file.

     463              : #define SLANG_PROCESSOR_FAMILY_ARM (SLANG_PROCESSOR_ARM | SLANG_PROCESSOR_ARM_64)
     464              : #define SLANG_PROCESSOR_FAMILY_POWER_PC (SLANG_PROCESSOR_POWER_PC_64 | SLANG_PROCESSOR_POWER_PC)
     465              : 
     466              : // Pointer size: prefer the compiler's own width macros, which are correct on every target
     467              : // (including architectures outside the SLANG_PROCESSOR_* whitelist below); the whitelist is only a
     468              : // fallback for toolchains that predefine none of them. MSVC exposes only _WIN64/_WIN32, and 64-bit
     469              : // Windows defines both, so _WIN64 is tested first.
     470              : #ifndef SLANG_PTR_IS_64
     471              :     #if defined(__LP64__) || defined(_LP64) || defined(_WIN64) || \
     472              :         (defined(__SIZEOF_POINTER__) && (__SIZEOF_POINTER__ == 8))
     473              :         #define SLANG_PTR_IS_64 1
     474              :     #elif defined(_WIN32) || (defined(__SIZEOF_POINTER__) && (__SIZEOF_POINTER__ == 4))
     475              :         #define SLANG_PTR_IS_64 0
     476              :     #elif (SLANG_PROCESSOR_ARM_64 | SLANG_PROCESSOR_X86_64 | SLANG_PROCESSOR_POWER_PC_64)
     477              :         #define SLANG_PTR_IS_64 1
     478              :     #elif (                                                                    \
     479              :         SLANG_PROCESSOR_ARM | SLANG_PROCESSOR_X86 | SLANG_PROCESSOR_POWER_PC | \
     480              :         SLANG_PROCESSOR_WASM)
     481              :         #define SLANG_PTR_IS_64 0
     482              :     #endif
     483              : #endif
     484              : 
     485              : // Fail loudly on an unresolved pointer size rather than silently choosing a 32-bit layout, which
     486              : // would corrupt SlangInt/SlangUInt and every struct laid out under SLANG_PTR_IS_32.
     487              : #ifndef SLANG_PTR_IS_64
     488              :     #error "Couldn't determine pointer size; define SLANG_PTR_IS_64 (0 or 1) via SLANG_USER_CONFIG."
     489              : #endif
     490              : 
     491              : // SLANG_PTR_IS_32 is derived as the boolean negation, so SLANG_PTR_IS_64 must be exactly 0 or 1;
     492              : // any other value (e.g. a stray user override of 2) would make both macros truthy.
     493              : #if (SLANG_PTR_IS_64 != 0) && (SLANG_PTR_IS_64 != 1)
     494              :     #error "SLANG_PTR_IS_64 must be defined to exactly 0 or 1."
     495              : #endif
     496              : 
     497              : #define SLANG_PTR_IS_32 (SLANG_PTR_IS_64 ^ 1)
     498              : 
     499              : #ifdef __cplusplus
     500              : // Cross-check the derived pointer size against what the compiler actually uses, so a wrong value
     501              : // fails at compile time instead of silently corrupting the ABI.
     502              : SLANG_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT((SLANG_PTR_IS_64 ? 8 : 4) == sizeof(void*));
     503              : #endif
     504              : 
     505              : // Endianness: prefer the compiler's own byte-order macros, correct on every target (including
     506              : // those outside the SLANG_PROCESSOR_* whitelist); MSVC predefines no __BYTE_ORDER__ but every
     507              : // Windows target is little-endian, so _WIN32 is special-cased, and the whitelist is only a
     508              : // fallback. Runs only when the user has forced neither macro.
     509              : #if !defined(SLANG_LITTLE_ENDIAN) && !defined(SLANG_BIG_ENDIAN)
     510              :     #if defined(__BYTE_ORDER__) && defined(__ORDER_LITTLE_ENDIAN__) && \
     511              :         (__BYTE_ORDER__ == __ORDER_LITTLE_ENDIAN__)
     512              :         #define SLANG_LITTLE_ENDIAN 1
     513              :     #elif defined(__BYTE_ORDER__) && defined(__ORDER_BIG_ENDIAN__) && \
     514              :         (__BYTE_ORDER__ == __ORDER_BIG_ENDIAN__)
     515              :         #define SLANG_BIG_ENDIAN 1
     516              :     #elif defined(_WIN32)
     517              :         #define SLANG_LITTLE_ENDIAN 1
     518              :     #elif SLANG_PROCESSOR_FAMILY_X86
     519              :         #define SLANG_LITTLE_ENDIAN 1
     520              :     #elif SLANG_PROCESSOR_FAMILY_ARM
     521              :         #if defined(__ARMEB__)
     522              :             #define SLANG_BIG_ENDIAN 1
     523              :         #else
     524              :             #define SLANG_LITTLE_ENDIAN 1
     525              :         #endif
     526              :     #elif SLANG_PROCESSOR_FAMILY_POWER_PC
     527              :         #define SLANG_BIG_ENDIAN 1
     528              :     #elif SLANG_WASM
     529              :         #define SLANG_LITTLE_ENDIAN 1
     530              :     #endif
     531              : #endif
     532              : 
     533              : // Unaligned memory access is a processor feature independent of byte order; only x86/x64 permit it.
     534              : #if SLANG_PROCESSOR_FAMILY_X86
     535              :     #define SLANG_UNALIGNED_ACCESS 1
     536              : #endif
     537              : 
     538              : #ifndef SLANG_LITTLE_ENDIAN
     539              :     #define SLANG_LITTLE_ENDIAN 0
     540              : #endif
     541              : 
     542              : #ifndef SLANG_BIG_ENDIAN
     543              :     #define SLANG_BIG_ENDIAN 0
     544              : #endif
     545              : 
     546              : #ifndef SLANG_UNALIGNED_ACCESS
     547              :     #define SLANG_UNALIGNED_ACCESS 0
     548              : #endif
     549              : 
     550              : // Backtrace
     551              : #if SLANG_LINUX_FAMILY
     552              :     #include <features.h> // for __GLIBC__ define, if using GNU libc
     553              :     #if defined(__GLIBC__) || (__ANDROID_API__ >= 33)
     554              :         #define SLANG_HAS_BACKTRACE 1
     555              :     #else
     556              :         #define SLANG_HAS_BACKTRACE 0
     557              :     #endif
     558              : #else
     559              :     #define SLANG_HAS_BACKTRACE 0
     560              : #endif
     561              : 
     562              : // Exactly one endianness must be set: neither means an undetected target, both means a
     563              : // contradictory user override that would leave consumers disagreeing.
     564              : #if !SLANG_BIG_ENDIAN && !SLANG_LITTLE_ENDIAN
     565              :     #error "Couldn't determine endianness"
     566              : #endif
     567              : #if SLANG_BIG_ENDIAN && SLANG_LITTLE_ENDIAN
     568              :     #error "Both SLANG_BIG_ENDIAN and SLANG_LITTLE_ENDIAN are set"
     569              : #endif
     570              : 
     571              : #ifndef SLANG_NO_INTTYPES
     572              :     #include <inttypes.h>
     573              : #endif // ! SLANG_NO_INTTYPES

     600              :     // Use SLANG_PTR_ macros to determine SlangInt/SlangUInt types.
     601              :     // This is used over say using size_t/ptrdiff_t/intptr_t/uintptr_t, because on some targets,
     602              :     // these types are distinct from their uint_t/int_t equivalents and so produce ambiguity with
     603              :     // function overloading.
     604              :     //
     605              :     // SlangSizeT is helpful as on some compilers size_t is distinct from a regular integer type and
     606              :     // so overloading doesn't work. Casting to SlangSizeT works around this.
     607              : #if SLANG_PTR_IS_64
     608              :     typedef int64_t SlangInt;
     609              :     typedef uint64_t SlangUInt;
     610              : 
     611              :     typedef int64_t SlangSSizeT;
     612              :     typedef uint64_t SlangSizeT;
     613              : #else
     614              : typedef int32_t SlangInt;
     615              : typedef uint32_t SlangUInt;
     616              : 
     617              : typedef int32_t SlangSSizeT;
     618              : typedef uint32_t SlangSizeT;

    1019              :     // All compiler option names supported by Slang.
    1020              :     //
    1021              :     // IMPORTANT: ABI STABILITY POLICY FOR CompilerOptionName
    1022              :     //
    1023              :     // Every enumerator except the terminal CountOf sentinel has an explicit integer value. Rules:
    1024              :     //   1. NEVER insert a new enumerator in the middle of the list.
    1025              :     //   2. NEVER remove an enumerator; rename to REMOVED_<Name> and keep the value.
    1026              :     //   3. NEVER reuse an integer value from a removed/deprecated entry.
    1027              :     //   4. NEW entries MUST be appended immediately before CountOf, assigning the next
    1028              :     //      sequential integer (equal to the preceding enumerator's value + 1).
    1029              :     //
    1030              :     // Violation of these rules silently breaks binary compatibility for any caller
    1031              :     // compiled against an older version of this header.
    1032              :     namespace slang
    1033              :     {

    1336              : 
    1337              :     /** A result code for a Slang API operation.
    1338              : 
    1339              :     This type is generally compatible with the Windows API `HRESULT` type. In particular, negative
    1340              :     values indicate failure results, while zero or positive results indicate success.
    1341              : 
    1342              :     In general, Slang APIs always return a zero result on success, unless documented otherwise.
    1343              :     Strictly speaking a negative value indicates an error, a positive (or 0) value indicates
    1344              :     success. This can be tested for with the macros SLANG_SUCCEEDED(x) or SLANG_FAILED(x).
    1345              : 
    1346              :     It can represent if the call was successful or not. It can also specify in an extensible manner
    1347              :     what facility produced the result (as the integral 'facility') as well as what caused it (as an
    1348              :     integral 'code'). Under the covers SlangResult is represented as a int32_t.
    1349              : 
    1350              :     SlangResult is designed to be compatible with COM HRESULT.
    1351              : 
    1352              :     It's layout in bits is as follows
    1353              : 
    1354              :     Severity | Facility | Code
    1355              :     ---------|----------|-----
    1356              :     31       |    30-16 | 15-0
    1357              : 
    1358              :     Severity - 1 fail, 0 is success - as SlangResult is signed 32 bits, means negative number
    1359              :     indicates failure. Facility is where the error originated from. Code is the code specific to the
    1360              :     facility.
    1361              : 
    1362              :     Result codes have the following styles,
    1363              :     1) SLANG_name
    1364              :     2) SLANG_s_f_name
    1365              :     3) SLANG_s_name
    1366              : 
    1367              :     where s is S for success, E for error
    1368              :     f is the short version of the facility name
    1369              : 
    1370              :     Style 1 is reserved for SLANG_OK and SLANG_FAIL as they are so commonly used.
    1371              : 
    1372              :     It is acceptable to expand 'f' to a longer name to differentiate a name or drop if unique
    1373              :     without it. ie for a facility 'DRIVER' it might make sense to have an error of the form
    1374              :     SLANG_E_DRIVER_OUT_OF_MEMORY
    1375              :     */
    1376              : 
    1377              :     typedef int32_t SlangResult;
    1378              : 
    1379              :     //! Use to test if a result was failure. Never use result != SLANG_OK to test for failure, as
    1380              :     //! there may be successful codes != SLANG_OK.
    1381         2244 : #define SLANG_FAILED(status) ((status) < 0)
    1382              :     //! Use to test if a result succeeded. Never use result == SLANG_OK to test for success, as will
    1383              :     //! detect other successful codes as a failure.
    1384          252 : #define SLANG_SUCCEEDED(status) ((status) >= 0)
    1385              : 
    1386              :     //! Get the facility the result is associated with
    1387              : #define SLANG_GET_RESULT_FACILITY(r) ((int32_t)(((r) >> 16) & 0x7fff))
    1388              :     //! Get the result code for the facility
    1389              : #define SLANG_GET_RESULT_CODE(r) ((int32_t)((r) & 0xffff))
    1390              : 
    1391              : #define SLANG_MAKE_ERROR(fac, code) \
    1392           18 :     ((((int32_t)(fac)) << 16) | ((int32_t)(code)) | int32_t(0x80000000))
    1393              : #define SLANG_MAKE_SUCCESS(fac, code) ((((int32_t)(fac)) << 16) | ((int32_t)(code)))
    1394              : 
    1395              :     /*************************** Facilities ************************************/
    1396              : 
    1397              :     //! Facilities compatible with windows COM - only use if known code is compatible
    1398              : #define SLANG_FACILITY_WIN_GENERAL 0
    1399              : #define SLANG_FACILITY_WIN_INTERFACE 4
    1400              : #define SLANG_FACILITY_WIN_API 7
    1401              : 
    1402              :     //! Base facility -> so as to not clash with HRESULT values (values in 0x200 range do not appear
    1403              :     //! used)
    1404              : #define SLANG_FACILITY_BASE 0x200
    1405              : 
    1406              :     /*! Facilities numbers must be unique across a project to make the resulting result a unique
    1407              :     number. It can be useful to have a consistent short name for a facility, as used in the name
    1408              :     prefix */
    1409              : #define SLANG_FACILITY_CORE SLANG_FACILITY_BASE
    1410              :     /* Facility for codes, that are not uniquely defined/protected. Can be used to pass back a
    1411              :     specific error without requiring system wide facility uniqueness. Codes should never be part of
    1412              :     a public API. */
    1413              : #define SLANG_FACILITY_INTERNAL SLANG_FACILITY_BASE + 1
    1414              : 
    1415              :     /// Base for external facilities. Facilities should be unique across modules.
    1416              : #define SLANG_FACILITY_EXTERNAL_BASE 0x210
    1417              : 
    1418              :     /* ************************ Win COM compatible Results ******************************/
    1419              :     // https://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/windows/desktop/aa378137(v=vs.85).aspx
    1420              : 
    1421              :     //! SLANG_OK indicates success, and is equivalent to

    1778              :     /** An extended file system abstraction.
    1779              : 
    1780              :     Implementing and using this interface over ISlangFileSystem gives much more control over how
    1781              :     paths are managed, as well as how it is determined if two files 'are the same'.
    1782              : 
    1783              :     All paths as input char*, or output as ISlangBlobs are always encoded as UTF-8 strings.
    1784              :     Blobs that contain strings are always zero terminated.
    1785              :     */
    1786              :     struct ISlangFileSystemExt : public ISlangFileSystem
    1787              :     {
    1788              :         SLANG_COM_INTERFACE(
    1789              :             0x5fb632d2,
    1790              :             0x979d,
    1791              :             0x4481,
    1792              :             {0x9f, 0xee, 0x66, 0x3c, 0x3f, 0x14, 0x49, 0xe1})
    1793              : 
    1794              :         /** Get a uniqueIdentity which uniquely identifies an object of the file system.
    1795              : 
    1796              :         Given a path, returns a 'uniqueIdentity' which ideally is the same value for the same object
    1797              :         on the file system.
    1798              : 
    1799              :         The uniqueIdentity is used to compare if two paths are the same - which amongst other things
    1800              :         allows Slang to cache source contents internally. It is also used for #pragma once
    1801              :         functionality.
    1802              : 
    1803              :         A *requirement* is for any implementation is that two paths can only return the same
    1804              :         uniqueIdentity if the contents of the two files are *identical*. If an implementation breaks
    1805              :         this constraint it can produce incorrect compilation. If an implementation cannot *strictly*
    1806              :         identify *the same* files, this will only have an effect on #pragma once behavior.
    1807              : 
    1808              :         The string for the uniqueIdentity is held zero terminated in the ISlangBlob of
    1809              :         outUniqueIdentity.
    1810              : 
    1811              :         Note that there are many ways a uniqueIdentity may be generated for a file. For example it
    1812              :         could be the 'canonical path' - assuming it is available and unambiguous for a file system.
    1813              :         Another possible mechanism could be to store the filename combined with the file date time
    1814              :         to uniquely identify it.
    1815              : 
    1816              :         The client must ensure the blob be released when no longer used, otherwise memory will leak.
    1817              : 
    1818              :         NOTE! Ideally this method would be called 'getPathUniqueIdentity' but for historical reasons
    1819              :         and backward compatibility it's name remains with 'File' even though an implementation
    1820              :         should be made to work with directories too.
    1821              : 
    1822              :         @param path
    1823              :         @param outUniqueIdentity
    1824              :         @returns A `SlangResult` to indicate success or failure getting the uniqueIdentity.
    1825              :         */
    1826              :         virtual SLANG_NO_THROW SlangResult SLANG_MCALL
    1827              :         getFileUniqueIdentity(const char* path, ISlangBlob** outUniqueIdentity) = 0;

    2246              :         // TODO: Ellie, Both APIs treat mesh outputs as more or less varying output,
    2247              :         // Does it deserve to be represented here??
    2248              : 
    2249              :         // A parameter whose type is to be specialized by a global generic type argument
    2250              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_GENERIC = 13,
    2251              : 
    2252              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_RAY_PAYLOAD = 14,
    2253              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_HIT_ATTRIBUTES = 15,
    2254              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_CALLABLE_PAYLOAD = 16,
    2255              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_SHADER_RECORD = 17,
    2256              : 
    2257              :         // An existential type parameter represents a "hole" that
    2258              :         // needs to be filled with a concrete type to enable
    2259              :         // generation of specialized code.
    2260              :         //
    2261              :         // Consider this example:
    2262              :         //
    2263              :         //      struct MyParams
    2264              :         //      {
    2265              :         //          IMaterial material;
    2266              :         //          ILight lights[3];
    2267              :         //      };
    2268              :         //
    2269              :         // This `MyParams` type introduces two existential type parameters:
    2270              :         // one for `material` and one for `lights`. Even though `lights`
    2271              :         // is an array, it only introduces one type parameter, because
    2272              :         // we need to have a *single* concrete type for all the array
    2273              :         // elements to be able to generate specialized code.
    2274              :         //
    2275              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_EXISTENTIAL_TYPE_PARAM = 18,
    2276              : 
    2277              :         // An existential object parameter represents a value
    2278              :         // that needs to be passed in to provide data for some
    2279              :         // interface-type shader parameter.
    2280              :         //
    2281              :         // Consider this example:
    2282              :         //
    2283              :         //      struct MyParams
    2284              :         //      {
    2285              :         //          IMaterial material;
    2286              :         //          ILight lights[3];
    2287              :         //      };
    2288              :         //
    2289              :         // This `MyParams` type introduces four existential object parameters:
    2290              :         // one for `material` and three for `lights` (one for each array
    2291              :         // element). This is consistent with the number of interface-type
    2292              :         // "objects" that are being passed through to the shader.
    2293              :         //
    2294              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_EXISTENTIAL_OBJECT_PARAM = 19,
    2295              : 
    2296              :         // The register space offset for the sub-elements that occupies register spaces.
    2297              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_SUB_ELEMENT_REGISTER_SPACE = 20,
    2298              : 
    2299              :         // The input_attachment_index subpass occupancy tracker
    2300              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_SUBPASS = 21,
    2301              : 
    2302              :         // Metal tier-1 argument buffer element [[id]].
    2303              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_METAL_ARGUMENT_BUFFER_ELEMENT = 22,
    2304              : 
    2305              :         // Metal [[attribute]] inputs.
    2306              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_METAL_ATTRIBUTE = 23,
    2307              : 
    2308              :         // Metal [[payload]] inputs
    2309              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_METAL_PAYLOAD = 24,
    2310              : 
    2311              :         //
    2312              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_COUNT,
    2313              : 
    2314              :         // Aliases for Metal-specific categories.
    2315              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_METAL_BUFFER = SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_CONSTANT_BUFFER,
    2316              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_METAL_TEXTURE = SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_SHADER_RESOURCE,
    2317              :         SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_METAL_SAMPLER = SLANG_PARAMETER_CATEGORY_SAMPLER_STATE,
    2318              : 
    2319              :         // DEPRECATED:

    2324              : 
    2325              :     /** Types of API-managed bindings that a parameter might use.
    2326              : 
    2327              :     `SlangBindingType` represents the distinct types of binding ranges that might be
    2328              :     understood by an underlying graphics API or cross-API abstraction layer.
    2329              :     Several of the enumeration cases here correspond to cases of `VkDescriptorType`
    2330              :     defined by the Vulkan API. Note however that the values of this enumeration
    2331              :     are not the same as those of any particular API.
    2332              : 
    2333              :     The `SlangBindingType` enumeration is distinct from `SlangParameterCategory`
    2334              :     because `SlangParameterCategory` differentiates the types of parameters for
    2335              :     the purposes of layout, where the layout rules of some targets will treat
    2336              :     parameters of different types as occupying the same binding space for layout
    2337              :     (e.g., in SPIR-V both a `Texture2D` and `SamplerState` use the same space of
    2338              :     `binding` indices, and are not allowed to overlap), while those same types
    2339              :     map to different types of bindings in the API (e.g., both textures and samplers
    2340              :     use different `VkDescriptorType` values).
    2341              : 
    2342              :     When you want to answer "what register/binding did this parameter use?" you
    2343              :     should use `SlangParameterCategory`.
    2344              : 
    2345              :     When you want to answer "what type of descriptor range should this parameter use?"
    2346              :     you should use `SlangBindingType`.
    2347              :     */

    3262              :     /** Retrieves a variable's default initializer as a packed byte blob.
    3263              :      *
    3264              :      * If the variable has no explicit initializer, returns `SLANG_OK` and sets `*outBlob` to
    3265              :      * `nullptr`. Otherwise `*outBlob` receives an `ISlangBlob*` with an added reference holding the
    3266              :      * initializer's bytes; the caller owns that reference. Returns `SLANG_E_INVALID_ARG` for null
    3267              :      * arguments and `SLANG_E_NOT_AVAILABLE` when the initializer cannot be represented as a
    3268              :      * default-value blob.
    3269              :      *
    3270              :      * Scalars, vectors, matrices, fixed-size arrays, structs/aggregates, and enums are supported.
    3271              :      * Values are packed in natural scalar/field order with no aggregate padding: matrices
    3272              :      * row-by-row, base-class fields before derived fields, and a field with no explicit initializer
    3273              :      * as its zero/default representation. Encoding is target-independent: `bool` occupies 4 bytes
    3274              :      * to match Slang's GPU scalar layout, `intptr_t`/`uintptr_t` always occupy 8 bytes
    3275              :      * signed/unsigned (consumers on narrower-pointer targets must narrow explicitly), and enums use
    3276              :      * their underlying tag type.
    3277              :      *
    3278              :      * Scalars are stored in host byte order (little-endian on all supported platforms), and the
    3279              :      * buffer is aligned to at least `alignof(max_align_t)`, which covers every scalar type encoded
    3280              :      * by this API. After checking the blob size, callers may cast `getBufferPointer()` directly to
    3281              :      * the payload element type.
    3282              :      */
    3283              :     SLANG_API SlangResult getDefaultValueBlob(ISlangBlob** outBlob);
    3284              :

    3893              :     /** Get the descriptor set/space index reserved for the bindless resource heap.
    3894              :      *
    3895              :      * This is a layout/reflection reservation made before final target lowering and
    3896              :      * optimization. It can remain non-negative even when the emitted target code no
    3897              :      * longer uses a bindless heap/resource-handle path. Query `IBindlessResourceMetadata`
    3898              :      * from target metadata to determine whether such a path survived in the compiled
    3899              :      * target IR.
    3900              :      *

    4065              : /** A global session for interaction with the Slang library.

    4327              :     /** Get the version of the downstream/pass-through compiler that Slang will actually load and
    4328              :     use for `passThrough`, applying the same lazy discovery and library search order used during
    4329              :     compilation. This lets a client key its behavior off the exact library Slang selected (for
    4330              :     example, the specific NVRTC that will compile CUDA), which can differ from a version the client
    4331              :     might discover on its own.
    4332              : 
    4333              :     This is not a cheap accessor: the first call for a given `passThrough` performs discovery and
    4334              :     loads the downstream library into the process (then memoizes it for subsequent calls).
    4335              : 
    4336              :     Only some downstream compilers report a numeric version (e.g. NVRTC, DXC, the C/C++ toolchains);
    4337              :     others (e.g. the glslang family and Tint) always report `(0,0)`. The version is read uniformly
    4338              :     from the loaded compiler's descriptor, so a versionless-but-loaded compiler still returns
    4339              :     SLANG_OK with major/minor 0 - which the result alone does not distinguish from a genuine 0.0.
    4340              :     @param passThrough The downstream compiler to query (e.g. SLANG_PASS_THROUGH_NVRTC).
    4341              :     @param outMajor Receives the major version number. May be null.
    4342              :     @param outMinor Receives the minor version number. May be null.
    4343              :     @return SLANG_OK if the compiler was located and loaded (see the versionless note above).
    4344              :     SLANG_E_NOT_FOUND if the compiler could not be located or loaded, and likewise for
    4345              :     SLANG_PASS_THROUGH_NONE or an out-of-range value - the result code alone does not distinguish an
    4346              :     invalid argument from a compiler that is simply not installed. */
    4347              :     virtual SLANG_NO_THROW SlangResult SLANG_MCALL
    4348              :     getDownstreamCompilerVersion(SlangPassThrough passThrough, int* outMajor, int* outMinor) = 0;
    4349              : };

    4465              : 
    4466              : /** A session provides a scope for code that is loaded.
    4467              : 
    4468              : A session can be used to load modules of Slang source code,
    4469              : and to request target-specific compiled binaries and layout
    4470              : information.
    4471              : 
    4472              : In order to be able to load code, the session owns a set
    4473              : of active "search paths" for resolving `#include` directives
    4474              : and `import` declarations, as well as a set of global
    4475              : preprocessor definitions that will be used for all code
    4476              : that gets `import`ed in the session.
    4477              : 
    4478              : If multiple user shaders are loaded in the same session,
    4479              : and import the same module (e.g., two source files do `import X`)
    4480              : then there will only be one copy of `X` loaded within the session.
    4481              : 
    4482              : In order to be able to generate target code, the session
    4483              : owns a list of available compilation targets, which specify
    4484              : code generation options.
    4485              : 
    4486              : Code loaded and compiled within a session is owned by the session
    4487              : and will remain resident in memory until the session is released.
    4488              : Applications wishing to control the memory usage for compiled
    4489              : and loaded code should use multiple sessions.
    4490              : */

    4518              :     be the union of those in `componentTypes`. The relative order of child
    4519              :     component types is significant, and will affect the order in which
    4520              :     parameters are reflected and laid out.

    4527              :     that can be satisfied by another entry, then the composition will
    4528              :     satisfy the requirement and it will not appear as a requirement of
    4529              :     the composite. If multiple entries in `componentTypes` have a requirement

    4531              :     on the composite. The relative ordering of requirements on the composite
    4532              :     will otherwise match that of `componentTypes`.

    4536              :     function will return null.

    4736              : /** Bindless resource metadata produced for a compiled target.
    4737              : 
    4738              : The bindless space index reported through program reflection is a frontend-predicted reserved
    4739              : descriptor space. It remains stable even when later optimization or target lowering removes all
    4740              : descriptor-handle heap use from the emitted shader. This metadata interface reports the
    4741              : post-lowering usage signal instead.
    4742              : 
    4743              : `usesBindlessResourceHeap()` reports whether the final target IR still contains the
    4744              : descriptor-handle/bindless resource path after target-specific lowering. This is a code-generation
    4745              : signal, not a complete cross-target host binding policy: targets that lower descriptor handles to
    4746              : native resource handles or addresses may not require an explicit descriptor-heap binding even when
    4747              : this returns true. Hosts should combine this query with their target binding model when deciding
    4748              : whether to bind a heap.
    4749              : 
    4750              : Cast from an artifact-associated `IMetadata*` using `castAs()`.
    4751              : */

    4767              :     #define SLANG_UUID_IBindlessResourceMetadata IBindlessResourceMetadata::getTypeGuid()
    4768              : 
    4769              : /** Coverage tracing metadata produced when any shader coverage mode is active.
    4770              : 
    4771              : The current implementation reports line, function-entry, and branch-arm
    4772              : hit-count coverage. Each emitted source entry carries the runtime
    4773              : counter slot that backs it. The interface lets hosts read that mapping
    4774              : at compile time so they can attribute counter values back to source
    4775              : locations at runtime without a separate sidecar file. The metadata is
    4776              : retrieved by calling `castAs` / `queryInterface` on the
    4777              : artifact-associated `IMetadata` object.
    4778              : 
    4779              : Intended use:
    4780              :   - use `ICoverageTracingMetadata` for coverage-specific semantics:
    4781              :     counter count, source-entry attribution, input to manifest
    4782              :     serialization, and future coverage-entry semantics
    4783              :   - use `ISyntheticResourceMetadata` for the generic hidden binding
    4784              :     contract when the host needs to bind compiler-synthesized resources
    4785              :     without relying on normal public reflection
    4786              :   - `getBufferInfo()` remains as a compatibility descriptor-binding
    4787              :     query for coverage-specific callers, but new host integrations
    4788              :     should prefer `ISyntheticResourceMetadata` because it also reports
    4789              :     CPU/CUDA marshaling locations
    4790              : 
    4791              : Lifetime and ownership:
    4792              :   - the metadata object is owned by the compiled artifact / `IMetadata`
    4793              :     container it is queried from
    4794              :   - pointers returned through `CoverageEntryInfo` remain valid for the
    4795              :     lifetime of that metadata object
    4796              :   - callers do not own returned strings and must not free them
    4797              : 
    4798              : Future coverage modes:
    4799              :   - this interface is intended to grow to cover richer reporting modes
    4800              :     such as source-region coverage, additional branch forms, binary
    4801              :     hit/not-hit counters, and warp/group-aggregated modes
    4802              :   - callers should not assume that future revisions will always model
    4803              :     one entry as one source line, one entry as one runtime counter, or
    4804              :     one counter value as the final reported count for a source range
    4805              : 
    4806              : Extensible without ABI breakage in two ways:
    4807              :   - tail-extending the `CoverageEntryInfo` struct (gated by its
    4808              :     leading `structSize` field), or
    4809              :   - adding a derived `ICoverageTracingMetadataN` interface with a
    4810              :     new UUID, queryable through `castAs`.
    4811              : 
    4812              : ABI note:
    4813              :   - this interface was extended in place while shader coverage still
    4814              :     had no known external implementors, to settle the source-entry
    4815              :     metadata model before broad adoption
    4816              :   - after this source-entry shape is externally consumed, future
    4817              :     vtable growth should use a derived/versioned interface instead
    4818              :     of appending methods to `ICoverageTracingMetadata`
    4819              : */
    4820              : enum class CoverageEntryKind : uint32_t
    4821              : {
    4822              :     Unknown = 0,
    4823              :     Line = 1,
    4824              :     Branch = 2,
    4825              :     Function = 3,
    4826              :     Region = 4,
    4827              : };
    4828              : 
    4829              : enum class CoverageCounterMode : uint32_t
    4830              : {
    4831              :     /// The counter holds the number of times the entry executed
    4832              :     /// (atomic add per execution).
    4833              :     Count = 0,
    4834              :     /// The counter is a boolean flag: `0` if the entry never executed,
    4835              :     /// non-zero if it executed at least once. Written with a plain
    4836              :     /// (non-atomic) store of `1`, so it carries no execution count but
    4837              :     /// avoids all atomic contention. Selected by
    4838              :     /// `-trace-coverage-boolean`.
    4839              :     Boolean = 1,
    4840              : };
    4841              :

    4843              : {
    4844              :     Unknown = 0,
    4845              :     TrueArm = 1,
    4846              :     FalseArm = 2,
    4847              :     CaseArm = 3,
    4848              :     DefaultArm = 4,

    4851              : inline constexpr uint32_t kInvalidCoverageCounterIndex = 0xffffffffu;
    4852              : 
    4853              : /// Per-coverage-entry attribution returned by
    4854              : /// `ICoverageTracingMetadata::getEntryInfo`. Use the leading
    4855              : /// `structSize` for ABI-versioned struct growth: future revisions
    4856              : /// may add fields such as column/span information, function identity,
    4857              : /// branch-arm identity, or coverage-mode-specific metadata at the end
    4858              : /// without changing the COM interface. Entries are source-location
    4859              : /// based: the current producers emit one source entry per marker op.
    4860              : /// If multiple line entries resolve to the same `(file, line)`, LCOV
    4861              : /// export aggregates them at report-generation time.
    4862              : struct CoverageEntryInfo
    4863              : {
    4864              :     size_t structSize = sizeof(CoverageEntryInfo);
    4865              : 
    4866              :     /// Source file for this coverage entry, or `nullptr` if the entry
    4867              :     /// could not be attributed to a real source file. The returned
    4868              :     /// pointer is valid for the lifetime of the metadata object.
    4869              :     const char* file = nullptr;
    4870              : 
    4871              :     /// 1-based source line for this coverage entry, or 0 if the entry
    4872              :     /// could not be attributed to a real source line. The current
    4873              :     /// implementation reports line, function, and branch entries;
    4874              :     /// future revisions may add source-region entries or additional
    4875              :     /// branch forms.
    4876              :     uint32_t line = 0;
    4877              : 
    4878              :     /// Counter slot used by this entry, or
    4879              :     /// `kInvalidCoverageCounterIndex` when the entry has no runtime
    4880              :     /// counter. The current line/function/branch producers use one
    4881              :     /// direct counter per entry. Future source-region coverage may use

    4929              : /// Coverage-buffer descriptor binding info returned by
    4930              : /// `ICoverageTracingMetadata::getBufferInfo`. This coverage-specific
    4931              : /// view is kept for ABI compatibility. New host integrations should
    4932              : /// query `ISyntheticResourceMetadata` when they need the full hidden-
    4933              : /// resource binding contract, including CPU/CUDA marshaling fields.
    4934              : ///
    4935              : /// Sentinel conventions:
    4936              : ///   - `space == -1` means no descriptor space is reported for the
    4937              : ///     current target
    4938              : ///   - `binding == -1` means no descriptor binding is reported for the
    4939              : ///     current target
    4940              : /// `0` is a valid value for both fields.
    4941              : struct CoverageBufferInfo
    4942              : {
    4943              :     size_t structSize = sizeof(CoverageBufferInfo);
    4944              : 
    4945              :     /// Register space the coverage buffer is bound to (D3D12
    4946              :     /// `space`, Vulkan descriptor set), or -1 if not assigned for
    4947              :     /// this target.
    4948              :     int32_t space = -1;
    4949              : 
    4950              :     /// Binding index the coverage buffer is bound at (D3D12
    4951              :     /// `register`, Vulkan `binding`), or -1 if not assigned for
    4952              :     /// this target.
    4953              :     int32_t binding = -1;
    4954              : 
    4955              :     /// Byte width of one counter slot in the synthesized buffer:
    4956              :     /// `4` for a `RWStructuredBuffer<uint>`, `8` for a
    4957              :     /// `RWStructuredBuffer<uint64_t>`. The host reads back
    4958              :     /// `getCounterCount() * elementByteWidth` bytes and interprets
    4959              :     /// each slot as a little-endian unsigned integer of this width.
    4960              :     /// Mirrored on the JSON sidecar as `buffer.element_stride`.
    4961              :     ///
    4962              :     /// A current in-process implementation always writes `4` or `8`;
    4963              :     /// the in-class default `4` only appears if the caller forgot to
    4964              :     /// pass the field to `getBufferInfo`. A sentinel `0` can only
    4965              :     /// arise when reading a metadata object from an older compiler
    4966              :     /// that pre-dates this field; both values should be treated as
    4967              :     /// the historical uint32 layout.
    4968              :     uint32_t elementByteWidth = 4;
    4969              : };

    5020              : /** Generic metadata for compiler-synthesized bindable resources.
    5021              : 
    5022              : This metadata is intended for features that inject hidden resources
    5023              : into the compiled program interface without surfacing them through the
    5024              : ordinary public reflection model. Shader coverage is the first
    5025              : consumer: its synthesized `__slang_coverage` buffer is reported here
    5026              : so hosts can discover how to bind it even though it is not part of
    5027              : normal `ProgramLayout` reflection.
    5028              : 
    5029              : The metadata is retrieved by calling `castAs` / `queryInterface` on the
    5030              : artifact-associated `IMetadata` object. Like coverage metadata, this
    5031              : interface is ABI-stable by design:
    5032              : 
    5033              :   - `SyntheticResourceInfo` may grow by tail extension guarded by its
    5034              :     leading `structSize`, or
    5035              :   - a future `ISyntheticResourceMetadataN` may be added with a new UUID.
    5036              : 
    5037              : Intended use:
    5038              :   - use `ISyntheticResourceMetadata` to discover and bind hidden
    5039              :     compiler-synthesized resources
    5040              :   - descriptor-backed hosts read `(space, binding)` from
    5041              :     `SyntheticResourceInfo`
    5042              :   - CPU/CUDA-style marshaling hosts read `uniformOffset` and
    5043              :     `uniformStride` from `SyntheticResourceInfo`
    5044              : 
    5045              : Lifetime and ownership:
    5046              :   - the metadata object is owned by the compiled artifact / `IMetadata`
    5047              :     container it is queried from
    5048              :   - pointers returned through `SyntheticResourceInfo` remain valid for
    5049              :     the lifetime of that metadata object
    5050              :   - callers do not own returned strings and must not free them
    5051              : 
    5052              : Thread safety:
    5053              :   - metadata objects are immutable once returned to the host; concurrent
    5054              :     read-only queries are allowed as long as callers keep the owning
    5055              :     COM object alive
    5056              : */
    5057              : enum class SyntheticResourceScope : uint32_t

    5059              :     /// One shared resource bound at program/global scope.
    5060              :     Global = 0,
    5061              : 
    5062              :     /// A resource scoped to one linked entry point.

    5064              : };

    5075              :     size_t structSize = sizeof(SyntheticResourceInfo);
    5076              : 
    5077              :     /// Stable, opaque, non-zero synthetic resource identifier within
    5078              :     /// the compiled program. `0` is reserved as the default
    5079              :     /// "unassigned" sentinel; `findResourceIndexByID(0, ...)` always
    5080              :     /// returns `SLANG_E_NOT_FOUND`. Non-zero ids are assigned from the

    5083              :     /// metadata and runtime binding helpers.

    5087              :     /// resource.

    5089              :

    5091              :     /// current instrumentation resources are scalar (`1`).
    5092              :     uint32_t arraySize = 1;
    5093              : 
    5094              :     /// Whether the resource is global/root-scoped or attached to a
    5095              :     /// specific entry point. Coverage currently reports a global
    5096              :     /// resource; entry-point scoped resources are reserved for future
    5097              :     /// synthetic-resource producers.
    5098              :     SyntheticResourceScope scope = SyntheticResourceScope::Global;
    5099              :

    5101              :     SyntheticResourceAccess access = SyntheticResourceAccess::Read;

    5104              :     /// No current coverage resource uses entry-point scope.
    5105              :     int32_t entryPointIndex = -1;
    5106              : 
    5107              :     /// Sentinel conventions:
    5108              :     ///   - `space == -1` means the target does not report a descriptor
    5109              :     ///     space for this resource
    5110              :     ///   - `binding == -1` means descriptor binding is unavailable for
    5111              :     ///     this target
    5112              :     ///   - `uniformOffset == -1` means CPU/CUDA-style marshaling
    5113              :     ///     location is unavailable for this target
    5114              :     ///   - `uniformStride == 0` means CPU/CUDA-style marshaling
    5115              :     ///     location is unavailable or no stride applies; when
    5116              :     ///     `uniformOffset >= 0`, a non-zero stride may be reported
    5117              :     ///     even for scalar resources

    5119              :     /// `uniformOffset`.
    5120              :     ///
    5121              :     /// Descriptor-facing location for backends that bind synthetic
    5122              :     /// resources via `(space, binding)`.
    5123              :     int32_t space = -1;
    5124              :     int32_t binding = -1;
    5125              : 
    5126              :     /// CPU/CUDA-style marshaling location in generated uniform /
    5127              :     /// wrapper parameter data, in bytes.
    5128              :     int32_t uniformOffset = -1;
    5129              : 
    5130              :     /// Byte stride between adjacent logical elements when
    5131              :     /// CPU/CUDA-style marshaling is reported.
    5132              :     int32_t uniformStride = 0;

    5293              : 
    5294              : /** A component type is a unit of shader code layout, reflection, and linking.
    5295              : 
    5296              : A component type is a unit of shader code that can be included into
    5297              : a linked and compiled shader program. Each component type may have:
    5298              : 
    5299              : * Zero or more uniform shader parameters, representing textures,
    5300              :   buffers, etc. that the code in the component depends on.
    5301              : 
    5302              : * Zero or more *specialization* parameters, which are type or
    5303              :   value parameters that can be used to synthesize specialized
    5304              :   versions of the component type.
    5305              : 
    5306              : * Zero or more entry points, which are the individually invocable
    5307              :   kernels that can have final code generated.
    5308              : 
    5309              : * Zero or more *requirements*, which are other component
    5310              :   types on which the component type depends.
    5311              : 
    5312              : One example of a component type is a module of Slang code:
    5313              : 
    5314              : * The global-scope shader parameters declared in the module are
    5315              :   the parameters when considered as a component type.
    5316              : 
    5317              : * Any global-scope generic or interface type parameters introduce
    5318              :   specialization parameters for the module.
    5319              : 
    5320              : * A module does not by default include any entry points when
    5321              :   considered as a component type (although the code of the
    5322              :   module might *declare* some entry points).
    5323              : 
    5324              : * Any other modules that are `import`ed in the source code
    5325              :   become requirements of the module, when considered as a
    5326              :   component type.
    5327              : 
    5328              : An entry point is another example of a component type:
    5329              : 
    5330              : * The `uniform` parameters of the entry point function are
    5331              :   its shader parameters when considered as a component type.
    5332              : 
    5333              : * Any generic or interface-type parameters of the entry point
    5334              :   introduce specialization parameters.
    5335              : 
    5336              : * An entry point component type exposes a single entry point (itself).
    5337              : 
    5338              : * An entry point has one requirement for the module in which
    5339              :   it was defined.
    5340              : 
    5341              : Component types can be manipulated in a few ways:
    5342              : 
    5343              : * Multiple component types can be combined into a composite, which
    5344              :   combines all of their code, parameters, etc.
    5345              : 
    5346              : * A component type can be specialized, by "plugging in" types and
    5347              :   values for its specialization parameters.
    5348              : 
    5349              : * A component type can be laid out for a particular target, giving

    5603              : /** A module is the granularity of shader code compilation and loading.
    5604              : 
    5605              : In most cases a module corresponds to a single compile "translation unit."
    5606              : This will often be a single `.slang` or `.hlsl` file and everything it
    5607              : `#include`s.
    5608              : 
    5609              : Notably, a module `M` does *not* include the things it `import`s, as these
    5610              : as distinct modules that `M` depends on. There is a directed graph of
    5611              : module dependencies, and all modules in the graph must belong to the
    5612              : same session (`ISession`).
    5613              : 
    5614              : A module establishes a namespace for looking up types, functions, etc.

    5733              : 
    5734              :     static SpecializationArg fromType(TypeReflection* inType)
    5735              :     {

    5738              :         rs.type = inType;

    5741              : 
    5742              :     static SpecializationArg fromExpr(const char* inExpr)
    5743              :     {
    5744              :         SpecializationArg rs;
    5745              :         rs.kind = Kind::Expr;
    5746              :         rs.expr = inExpr;
    5747              :         return rs;
    5748              :     }
